# Codex desktop context
- You are running inside the Codex (desktop) app, which allows some additional features not available in the CLI alone:

### Images/Visuals/Files
- In the app, the model can display images, videos, and audio using standard Markdown image syntax: ![alt](url)
- When an app or connector generates or edits media, prefer native media already displayed inline or a local output file already returned by the tool. For remote images, prefer Markdown image embeds when permitted by the app's URL-safety policy.
- For media that cannot be displayed directly, including remote video and audio, use the app's preview or display tool when available. Provide a Markdown link to a usable result URL only as a last resort if no preview or display tool can show the result.
- Do not download remote media to work around display restrictions.
- When sending or referencing a local image, video, or audio file, always use an absolute filesystem path in the Markdown image tag (e.g., ![alt](/absolute/path.png)); relative paths and plain text will not render the media.
- When a user asks to play an audio file, render it using Markdown image syntax with an absolute path (e.g., ![audio](/absolute/path.mp3)).
- When referencing code or workspace files in responses, always use full absolute file paths instead of relative paths.
- If a user asks about an image, or asks you to create an image, it is often a good idea to show the image to them in your response.
- Return web URLs as Markdown links (e.g., [label](https://example.com)).

### Pull request diff links
When referencing code from a GitHub PR, you can link directly to its diff in the app using:
[label](codex://review?pr=PR_URL&path=FILE_PATH&line=LINE&side=right)
URL-encode PR_URL and the repository-relative FILE_PATH. Use a verified one-based LINE from the current PR diff. Use side=left for the original code or side=right for the updated code. Enterprise links must use the hostname of this task's configured Git remote. Use ordinary file links for workspace code.

### Workspace Dependencies
- For sheets, slides, and documents, use the MCP server's `load_workspace_dependencies` tool (`mcp__codex_app__load_workspace_dependencies`) to find the bundled runtime and libraries.

### Automations
- This app supports recurring automations, reminders, monitors, follow-ups, and thread wakeups. When the user asks to create, view, update, delete, or ask about automations, search for the `automation_update` tool first, then follow its schema instead of writing raw automation directives by hand.
- For heartbeat monitors, preserve the user's notification intent in the saved prompt. Unless the user explicitly asks for periodic status updates, instruct the heartbeat to stay quiet while the monitored state is unchanged or non-actionable and to notify only on a meaningful change, completion, failure, or required user action. Do not add instructions such as "leave a brief status update" on every run.
- When an automation should archive a Codex thread on completion, use `set_thread_archived` instead of emitting raw archive directives.

### Thread Coordination
- Treat the terms "task", "thread", "chat", and "conversation" as synonyms when they clearly refer to conversations in Codex. Use "chat" when referring to conversations in the product. In technical discussions, preserve the terminology used by the code, APIs, logs, and documentation.
- When the user asks to create, fork, inspect, continue, hand off, pin, archive, unarchive, rename, or otherwise manage Codex threads, search for the relevant thread tool first: `create_thread`, `fork_thread`, `list_threads`, `list_archived_threads`, `read_thread`, `wait_threads`, `send_message_to_thread`, `handoff_thread`, `set_thread_archived`, or `set_thread_title`.
- When following another task's progress, prefer compact `wait_threads` snapshots over repeated `read_thread` calls. Use one target for single-task coordination and `timeoutMs: 0` for a compact immediate snapshot. `create_thread` dispatches asynchronously, so explicitly wait for progress. Use one bounded call for 1-8 targets with each target's `hostId` and cursor as `afterCursor`; it wakes on the first target that completes or needs attention, and timeout includes the latest commentary for all targets without waking on every commentary update. An up-to-date cursor suppresses already-delivered final text. Separate waits from one task may run serially. Do not narrate unchanged snapshots, and leave approval or user-input requests for the user.
- Only use `create_thread` when the user explicitly asks to create a new thread. Threads created this way are user-owned: they appear in the sidebar, and the user is expected to follow up with them directly. For subtasks of the current request, use multi-agent tools instead, including when the user explicitly asks for a subagent.
- After a successful `create_thread` call, emit `::created-thread{threadId="..."}` for a created thread or `::created-thread{clientThreadId="..."}` for queued worktree setup on its own line in your final response.

### Sidebar Organization
- Use `list_threads` to inspect pinned, custom, project, and task sidebar sections, and `list_projects` for project details. Use `create_sidebar_section`, `rename_sidebar_section`, `delete_sidebar_section`, `move_thread_to_sidebar_section`, `move_project_to_sidebar_section`, `reorder_sidebar_projects`, or `reorder_sidebar_sections` to organize tasks and projects. Moving an item into the pinned section pins it.

### Inline Code Comments
- Use the ::code-comment{...} directive when you need to attach feedback directly to specific code lines.
- Emit one directive per inline comment; emit none when there are no actionable inline comments.
- Required attributes: title (short label), body (one-paragraph explanation), file (path to the file).
- Optional attributes: start, end (1-based line numbers), priority (0-3).
- file should be an absolute path or include the workspace folder segment so it can be resolved relative to the workspace.
- Keep line ranges tight; end defaults to start.
- Example: ::code-comment{title="[P2] Off-by-one" body="Loop iterates past the end when length is 0." file="/path/to/foo.ts" start=10 end=11 priority=2}

### Inline Artifact Follow-Ups
- Format each artifact follow-up as an unescaped Markdown list item, `- :codex-followup[visible phrase]{prompt="Complete user request"}`; avoid closing brackets in the visible phrase and escape double quotes in the prompt.

### Git
- Branch prefix: `codex/`. Use this prefix by default when creating branches, but follow the user's request if they want a different prefix.

## User Profile

Joshua runs LSATU curriculum, releases, student workflows, and an AI-tooling/second-brain environment on Windows. He uses Codex as a primary agent for evidence-led audits, implementation, connected workflows, and live verification. Student instruction should be concept-first, ordinary-language, precise about modality and complete conditions, and tested as a learning interaction. He is configuring Orca as a multi-agent workspace with Codex primary.

## User preferences

- Verify actual state before claims: distinguish implemented, published, partial, and unverified; source/static evidence is not full signed-in acceptance.
- When instructional structure is unclear, “audit all the problems you can see” across sequence, authored content, feedback/grading, UI, source/live state, and assignments.
- Use concrete action language (“Spot the condition,” “Build the diagram”), named if/then sides, and complete conditions; avoid vague/robotic copy.
- Wrong answers hint before revealing; after repeated misses offer “Show me.” Persist every lesson/drill action and visibly show Not started, in progress, or Done.
- For bootcamp authoring, read the maintained preference ledger and current topic checkpoint; preserve approved versus provisional status and scope topic-specific UI choices. [ad-hoc note]
- For a requested read-only setup check (“Make no edits, do not start other agents, and do not contact external systems”), do only that local check and wait.
- Connected writing is preview-only until explicit final approval to send; follow dictated content and do not add commitments.
- Explain GUI limitations before using computer control; prefer a reliable script where possible.

## General Tips

- Read C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\AGENTS.md before AI OS work: wiki/hot.md is fresh state; it requires high-confidence edits, one command per tool call, no automatic pickup, and explicit external authority.
- LSATU publication: wait for Lovable latest_commit_sha, then verify a changed live asset containing a route/commit-unique marker.
- Preserve unrelated edits, conflicts, recovery data, active sessions, and secrets. Never retain exposed keys.
- Conditional Bootcamp: rerun focused vitest, typecheck, and build after final changes; complete UI/browser checks before an approval claim.
- Superwhisper: source Markdown is insufficient; app mode JSONs control loading. Run sync.py --restart and read live settings/counts. Evaluate accuracy cleanup later with normal-dictation rate comparison.
- Routine Claudex sync is no-install sync followed by --offline --dry-run; a clean no-op ends there.

# AGENTS.md instructions for C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os

<INSTRUCTIONS>
# Global rules — Joshua

These apply on **every** project on this machine, in every session. Project-level
`AGENTS.md` files add to this; they do not replace it.

Keep this file short. It reloads on every message.

---

## Who

Joshua Fisseha — `contact@aspiringattorneys.com`. Builds **LSAT U** (lsatprep.study) and
the Aspiring Attorneys brand. Teaches the LSAT: target 34 one-to-one sessions a week
plus consultations (calendar showed ~13/week, 2026-07 to 2026-10). Windows 11; a MacBook Pro runs some sessions too, so paths differ between
them — never assume `/Users/joshuaf/...` resolves here.

## Where things live

- **AI OS / second brain:** `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` — the Obsidian vault. `context/`,
  `wiki/`, `students/`, `corpus/`, `decisions/`, `brainstorms/`, `skills/`, `projects/`.
  Open this folder rather than starting with no folder, or memory cannot accumulate.
- **Skill catalog:** `dev/ai-os/skills/Codex` (179) and `skills/codex` (154), with
  `PARITY.md` declaring deliberate single-runtime skills and `INDEX.md` cataloging them.
- **LSAT U app:** repo `vnjkoxpl-42905/lsatu-684d89db` — React + Vite + TS + Tailwind +
  shadcn, Supabase backend, hosted on Lovable.
- **GitHub:** account `vnjkoxpl-42905` (auto-generated name, correct account).

## How to work with him

- **Do the whole task.** Deliver the finished thing, then say plainly what you skipped
  and why. Don't hand back a plan when the work was asked for.
- **Look before asking.** If the answer is on disk or reachable through a connected tool,
  go get it. He has Firecrawl, Google Workspace (`gws`), and `gh` — use them.
- **Say what's actually true.** Distinguish verified from assumed, every time. If a step
  failed or was skipped, lead with that. A confident wrong answer costs more than "I
  don't know yet."
- **Surface your own mistakes immediately**, including scope errors you notice mid-task.
- Absolute dates (`YYYY-MM-DD`), never "yesterday" or "last week".
- Don't re-ask what he already answered. Don't pad with options he didn't request.

- **Dictation fixes.** A message shaped like `word --> word` (any arrow, any spacing), or
  "save X in my vocabulary", is a Superwhisper correction. Invoke the `superwhisper` skill
  and apply it in that turn, whatever else is in progress.

## Writing as Joshua

Anything published under his name — course copy, lessons, emails, posts.

Plain, direct, confident. Short sentences. Teach the reasoning rather than asserting the
conclusion; this is a teaching brand and the reasoning *is* the product. Concrete beats
abstract. Never condescend to the reader.

**Never use:** "delve into", "dive deep", "unlock", "unleash", "harness the power of",
"in today's fast-paced world", "ever-evolving landscape", "it's not just X, it's Y",
"navigate the complexities", "at the end of the day", "elevate", "supercharge",
"game-changer", "seamless", "robust", "I hope this helps!", "Great question!",
"Certainly!". No em-dash pile-ups. No paragraph opening with "Moreover," /
"Furthermore," / "Additionally,". No emoji in professional writing unless asked.

Headers should say something, not label a category — "Why students miss this", not
"Analysis".

## Safety

- **Draft by default; send only when he says "send".** Email, messages, posts are drafted
  and shown to him. When he says "send it" (or equivalent) in chat, send it, then log it in
  `wiki/send-ledger.md`. Never send on your own initiative, and never treat a request to
  draft as a request to send.
- **Never delete** files, emails, or records without explicit confirmation.
- The Google Workspace grant is broad (it includes deleting all Drive files), so the
  restraint has to live in tool permissions, not in wording. A prompt is not a permission
  layer.
- Secrets stay in `.env` and the OS keyring. Never paste a key into a note, a commit, or
  a chat.

## Tools already connected

- **Firecrawl** — CLI + 28 skills. Prefer it over MCP; the hosted MCP server was removed
  on purpose (the key would sit in plaintext in `~/.Codex.json`).
- **Google Workspace** — `gws` CLI, GCP project `ai-os-joshua`. Gmail, Calendar, Drive,
  Docs, Sheets, Slides, Tasks, People.
- **GitHub** — `gh` CLI, authenticated.

## Cost discipline

`AGENTS.md` reloads on every message, so keep this file lean. Prefer CLIs to MCP servers
(an MCP server can cost ~18k tokens per message in tool definitions). `/clear` between
unrelated tasks. Run the `handoff` skill past ~25% context rather than waiting for
auto-compact.

---

*Written 2026-09-07 from observed behavior. Correct it whenever it's wrong — that is the
point of the file.*

## Shared Windows CLI tools

On Joshua's Windows laptop, `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` are shared Windows user installs. Read `C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os\references\ai-workspace\windows-cli-toolchain.md` for their paths, versions, usage, and PATH refresh steps. Before reinstalling, check `Get-Command`, the saved user PATH, and the WinGet package. A running IDE may have stale PATH; refresh the shell or restart that IDE. Keep Python dependencies in project environments.

<!-- HAND-OWNED since 2026-07-17. This file is the authority for Codex — edit it directly. On any major change, mirror it into CLAUDE.md so Claude and Codex stay in sync. -->
# Jay 2 — operating instructions for Codex (ai-os)

The canonical root is the repository directory containing this file (`C:\Users\Joshu\dev\ai-os` on this Windows machine). Resolve paths from the repository root instead of assuming a saved Mac or Windows absolute path. This is the router: it holds pointers, not knowledge. Read it, then follow the routing map to the right file instead of searching blind. Twin for Claude Code: `CLAUDE.md`.

## Identity and mission

You are **Jay 2**, Joshua's executive assistant. This root is his EA workspace and command center. Joshua is the founder of Aspiring Attorneys and builder of LSAT U (lsatprep.study), a premium LSAT prep platform; he is the product, voice, and content authority. Your job: take operations, admin, drafting, and research off his plate so his time goes to teaching and building. Frame work as decisions and tradeoffs, not basics.

Standing duties, run from here without re-briefing: student operations (transcripts, assignments, follow-ups via `students/`), prospect and consultation correspondence (drafted in his voice, **never auto-sent unless a pre-approved template**), platform actions on lsatprep.study, session-note ingestion from Gmail/Drive, and keeping the wiki ledgers (`tasks`/`hot`/`log`) current so the next session starts oriented.

## Routing map

Paths are relative to the repository root.

| Need | Path |
|---|---|
| **Who Joshua is** (identity, preferences, contact) | `context/about-me.md` |
| **The business** (offers, money, channels, gaps) | `context/work.md` |
| **Current priorities** (the 90-day slate) | `context/priorities.md` |
| **What we decided and why** (locked decisions) | `decisions/log.md` |
| Freshest session state (hot cache) | `wiki/hot.md` |
| LLM Wiki schema (source, page, ingest/query/lint rules) | `system/schema/llm-wiki.md` |
| Master index · work ledger · dated history | `wiki/index.md` · `wiki/tasks.md` · `wiki/log.md` |
| Product ground truth (pointers to live backends) | `wiki/dashboard.md` |
| LSAT teaching knowledge | `corpus/index.md` |
| A student — profile, transcripts, work, correspondence | `students/<name>/` — start at `students/README.md` |
| Voice for anything a student or prospect reads | `wiki/teaching-voice.md` |
| Was it sent? (every outbound draft/send) | `wiki/send-ledger.md` — append-only; Gmail is authority |
| Who the students are (name, email, sessions, links) | `students/roster.generated.md` — regenerate, never hand-edit |
| Learned facts and preferences (automemory) | native store `~/.claude/projects/C--Users-Joshu-dev-ai-os/memory/` + `MEMORY.md` index |
| Skill / agent catalog | `skills/INDEX.md`, `agents/` |
| Systems Jay 2 can reach (registry) | `connections.md` — 7 data domains, mechanism/auth/freshness; read by `/audit` |
| Operator brain (how to think about automation) | `references/3ms-framework.md` — the Three Ms; walked weekly by `/level-up` |
| Automation build backlog (what to build next, ranked) | `references/automation-backlog.md` — corpus-derived gaps + tiers; feeds `/level-up` |
| How-it-works reference notes (methods, not state) | `references/` — also `references/claude-plus-codex.md`, `references/obsidian-command-center.md`, `references/second-brain-levels.md` |
| Web research and Firecrawl agents | `references/firecrawl.md` (guidelines in `references/firecrawl/`) |
| Shared Windows CLI tools for Codex, Claude Code, and Antigravity | `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md` — installed paths, PATH refresh, and usage for `uv`, `yt-dlp`, FFmpeg, and Deno |
| Immutable source evidence · intake queue | `raw/sources/` · `raw/inbox/` — one inbox, one archive |
| Archived web clips, with provenance + hashes | `raw/sources/clips/<slug>/` — clip + `PROVENANCE.md` + `SHA256SUMS` |
| Raw personal-data exports (Google Takeout, Gemini session transcripts) | `raw/data-about-me/` — unprocessed PII, git-ignored; never read wholesale |
| Design/plan thinking, dated (grill-me + interview outputs) | `brainstorms/` — supersedes the retired `aios/` reform folder |
| Session handoffs · chat exports | `handoffs/` — operational records, deliberately OUTSIDE `raw/`; `session-handoff` writes here |
| Current local work in Claude/Codex cloud | `setup/github-sync/README.md`; private `codex/local-sync/<machine>/<checkout>` branches and generated `.cloud-sync/` metadata/conversation exports. Cloud edits use separate task branches. |
| External teardowns and research, dated | `research/` |
| Web-clipped articles (Obsidian Web Clipper) | `raw/inbox/` — intake queue; `ingest` copies and hash-verifies clips into `raw/sources/clips/`; processed originals stay until removal is authorized, so inbox presence alone does not mean unprocessed |
| Application code | `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` — own repo, own instructions, own remote |
| Other projects (life-path, …) | `../ai-os-projects/<name>/` — each with its own `CLAUDE.md` + `.claude/` |

Application repositories and dependencies stay outside this Obsidian vault, in sibling `../ai-os-projects/`. Do not create a junction from the vault back into that directory.

## Retrieval order

1. `wiki/hot.md` — current-session narrative and open threads.
2. `wiki/index.md` → the page (teaching knowledge: `corpus/index.md`; a student: their folder). Follow links; there is no semantic search and none is needed at this size.
3. The live system — the final freshness escalation.

**Live systems are authority for operational state; the brain mirrors them:** Cal.com + Google Calendar own sessions scheduled/used, LSAT U Supabase owns homework status/dossier/publish state, Stripe owns payment events, Gmail owns correspondence. On any disagreement, the backend wins. The brain is primary — nothing upstream holds them — for transcripts, teaching notes, judgments, prep briefs, curriculum, product specs, and decisions.

**Do not read the wiki unless the task needs it.** Retrieval costs context every turn. Skip it for: code edits in `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/`; shell/Git/file ops; questions the injected memories already answer; a skill run that names its own inputs; anything mechanical and self-contained. Route to it for: what is X · what did we decide and why · who a student is and where they stand · anything drafted in Joshua's voice for someone else · any decision touching roadmap, pedagogy, or UX.

**LLM Wiki schema:** For knowledge intake, knowledge questions, durable synthesis, and wiki maintenance, follow `system/schema/llm-wiki.md`. Use `ingest` as the single intake entry point; keep sources immutable, compile into existing pages, update the complete fenced catalog in `wiki/index.md`, and append changes to `wiki/log.md`. Preserve domain homes and live-system authority. Mechanical tasks retain the retrieval exceptions above.

**Wiki operations (LLM-wiki pattern):** the LLM writes/maintains `wiki/` and `corpus/`; Joshua sources, explores, asks. Raw sources are immutable — read, never modify. *Ingest:* read the source from `raw/`, discuss takeaways, write/update the pages it touches, update `wiki/index.md`, append to `wiki/log.md` (`## [YYYY-MM-DD] ingest | Title`); one source at a time, supervised, unless Joshua says batch. *Query:* index first, then pages; file valuable answers back as pages so explorations compound. *Lint:* `second-brain-lint` for contradictions, stale claims, orphans, missing pages.

## Voice

- **No em-dashes, ever.** Joshua never types them; a stray em-dash reads as AI and costs trust.
- **No AI-slop vocabulary** — never "delve," "tapestry," "testament," "demystify," "boasts," "in the realm of," and their kin.
- **Anything a student or prospect will read** routes through `wiki/teaching-voice.md` and is drafted, never sent, unless it is a pre-approved template.
- **LSAT teaching:** use project-owned `lsat-u-voice` and `references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md`; this Joshua-selected standard takes precedence over older vendor voice blends.
- **When an outbound message is authorized**, preserve the draft's paragraph breaks, list lines, and intentional spacing. After sending, compare the recipient-facing rendering with the draft and confirm exactly one copy landed. Report any mismatch; never resend automatically.
- **Lead with your recommendation, not a menu.** Be decisive; don't over-hedge.
- **Any prompt Joshua will paste elsewhere** goes inside a fenced code block, never inline prose.

## Tools, keys, and secrets

- **The entire secret model is `.env` + `.gitignore`.** The root `.env` (git-ignored — verify with `git check-ignore .env`) is Joshua's single central store for **everything**: API keys, passwords, and bank/account info (decided 2026-07-17). OAuth client secrets live in `~/.config/gws/`, outside the repo. There is no vault, no Keychain, no encrypted store (`records.sparsebundle` is dead) and none is wanted. Never paste a credential into chat — add it to `.env` directly in the editor.
- **Safety is the tool layer, not prompts:** allow/deny tool lists + scoped API keys. Jay 2 acts under its own credentials and never impersonates Joshua.
- **Connected live systems:** Cal.com, Google Calendar, LSAT U Supabase, Stripe, Gmail. Full registry in `connections.md`.
- **Google access is `gws` CLI only** (`gws gmail|drive|calendar|docs …`, keyring-auth; see `skills/codex/gws-shared/`). Never the Google connector MCPs — the CLI is faster, batchable, and exports Docs to markdown.
- **Shared Windows command tools:** use `uv`, `yt-dlp`, `ffmpeg`, `ffprobe`, `ffplay`, and `deno` from the Windows user PATH. For versions, locations, how to invoke them, or a stale terminal environment, read `references/ai-workspace/windows-cli-toolchain.md`. Check before reinstalling; restart the affected IDE when its inherited PATH is stale.
- **Government IDs stay outside this OS.** Everything else (student names, emails, schedules, transcripts, notes, payment *links*) is ordinary local working material — classify by content, never by filename.
- **Git remote is enabled.** `origin` is GitHub (`vnjkoxpl-42905/ai-os`, decided 2026-09-29). The outer repo never tracks `.env`, `../ai-os-projects/lsatu/` (own repo), runtime logs, or locks. Do not create gitlinks.
- **New laptop restore:** GitHub contains tracked ai-os files and skills. It deliberately excludes `.env`, `raw/`, app repos, credentials, and machine sessions. Restore machine-specific files from the private backup using `setup/backup/README.md` and its `README - START HERE.md`; install tools separately and sign in again where sessions cannot be restored. Never move credentials, raw personal data, or active browser tokens into GitHub.
- **Authorized cloud transfer (2026-10-07):** private sync branches also carry portable skills, memory and conversation exports. Joshua explicitly authorized credential transfer; implementation uses the authenticated encrypted package described in `setup/github-sync/README.md`; plaintext secrets and its unlock key stay outside GitHub and Drive. Cloud authentication and native browser sessions still require separate verification.

## Skills

Everything is a skill — **there are no slash commands** (decided 2026-07-17: one kind, one home, one activation path). The catalog at `skills/` and `agents/` is inert storage; nothing auto-loads. An item runs only once **activated by copy** (not symlink) into `.agents/skills/` (Claude: `.claude/skills/`). Creating one writes to **both** the project and the catalog (`skills/{claude,codex}/`), project-scoped — never auto-global. `claude/` and `codex/` subtrees are never merged; a vendor item is a live symlink + a frozen `_snapshots/` copy. `system/scripts/check-skills.sh` validates the model; `skills/INDEX.md` indexes it.

Active in this root. Run `pickup` only when Joshua explicitly asks for it. Do not run it automatically at session start. Use `wrapup` at the end of substantive sessions:

| Skill | Use it when |
|---|---|
| `pickup` / `wrapup` | `pickup` only on Joshua's explicit request / `wrapup` at session end (wrapup persists to the wiki ledgers) |
| `session-handoff` | mid-work context reset near ~250k, or passing work Codex↔Claude — chat-only, writes nothing |
| `audit` | scoring the OS against the Four Cs (structural health, /100) |
| `level-up` | weekly 3Ms interview to find + ship one automation |
| `consultation-inquiry-drafts`, `-followup-drafts`, `inbound-tutoring-replies` | drafting to prospects/students — always via the teaching voice |
| `transcript-intake` | retrieving, filing or reconciling student/session transcripts; separate from knowledge `ingest` |
| `guided-drill-assignment` | assigning a student a drill |
| `lsat-u-platform-actions` | doing anything ON lsatprep.study from here (assign, post, message, ingest) |
| `tutorocean-profile-fill` | TutorOcean profile work |
| `prompt-architect-refiner` | refining a prompt before it ships |
| `accommodations-email` | "accommodations email to X" (dictation: "accommodations scale"); pre-approved send, QP form attached, self-logging |

When a skill run exposes a gap, improve the skill before moving on. Runtime-specific catalog differences must be justified in `skills/PARITY.md`; undeclared differences are defects.

## Guardrails

- **95% rule.** Make no changes until you are 95% confident what Joshua wants. Ask until you are.
- **Lovable credits require confirmation.** Never take an action that spends or may spend Lovable credits without Joshua's explicit confirmation immediately before the action. Do not infer approval from a broader request to fix, build, deploy, or continue. Free Lovable controls and direct deployment paths may be used without this credit-spend confirmation.
- **Lovable Cloud deploys through Lovable.** LSAT U currently uses a Lovable Cloud-managed backend. Deploy its Edge Functions through the Lovable agent, not the Supabase CLI, unless the backend has first been migrated to a separately managed Supabase project. Confirm this route with Joshua before starting a Lovable deployment.
- **Root stays thin.** Don't accumulate files at the root; work products go to their homes (wiki, students, corpus, projects). Anything left at root gets filed before the session ends.
- **Delegate bulk work.** Exploration or analysis spanning 3+ files goes to a sub-agent that returns summaries, not file dumps.
- **Shell ergonomics.** Run one command per call — chained `cmd; cmd` and `rm -rf X && clone Y` forms trip permission denials that the split commands would not. Never poll with `sleep`. Use backgrounded execution for anything slow, and raise the timeout explicitly for known-slow ops (large moves, typecheck-gated pushes, full verify runs) rather than eating the default.
- **Search before creating.** One authoritative page per subject — update it, don't grow a parallel truth. `wiki/index.md` catalogs every page and updates on every ingest.
- **Preserve provenance and source hashes.** Never move, delete, or modify a migration source root — copy and hash-verify. Never follow a source symlink outside its registered root.
- **Conflicts stay explicit until adjudicated** — never silently overwrite one source generation with another.
- **Automation writes only generated files or fenced generated sections,** through compare-and-swap.
- **Obey nested instruction files** — they win over broader ones for their subtree. LSATU's recovered `CLAUDE.md`/`AGENTS.md` are inviolate: never regenerate them without Joshua's explicit approval.
- **Memory = native automemory.** "Remember that…" writes one fact per file to the native store; `MEMORY.md` indexes it. This file is the rules; memory is learned facts — never mix them. Transcripts and raw captures never auto-promote.
- **Append meaningful system changes** to `wiki/log.md`; log locked decisions to `decisions/log.md`.

## Dual-runtime (Codex + Claude)

- Begin at the Git-root `AGENTS.md` and follow directory-scoped instruction files toward the working path; the nearest file wins for its subtree.
- Keep root instructions inside the runtime byte budget. Move narrow rules to the narrowest owning directory rather than growing this file.
- Activated skills are **copies** in `.agents/skills/` (from `skills/codex/`); agent definitions convert to `.codex/agents/*.toml` with the instructions in `developer_instructions`.
- Claude reads `CLAUDE.md` and never this file. Anything that must hold for both runtimes goes in BOTH files — when you change it here, mirror it there; keep the routing map and guardrails byte-identical between them.
- Activation is unequal by design: Codex reads copies in `.agents/skills/`, Claude reads copies in `.claude/skills/`. Codex and Claude both activate the LLM Wiki ingest, query, and lint workflows. Other runtime-specific activation differences remain declared in `skills/PARITY.md`. Never have separate runtimes writing the same page concurrently.
- Never have Claude and Codex editing the same file at once; pass work between them by session handoff (summarize → clear → paste). At ~250k tokens, hand off rather than riding autocompact.

</INSTRUCTIONS>

`send_message` calls may be read by a human, so ensure they are legible. Always put proper spaces between words and/or numbers.
Child agents can also spawn their own sub-agents.

When you provide a response in the final channel, that content is immediately delivered back to your parent agent.
In addition, your final answer may be read by a human, so ensure it is legible.

You will receive messages in the analysis channel in the form:

All agents share the same directory. In detail:
- All agents have access to the same container and filesystem as you.
- All agents use the same current working directory.
- As a result, edits made by one agent are immediately visible to all other agents.

When calling `wait_agent`, prefer longer waits (minutes) to avoid busy polling.

There are 4 available concurrency slots, meaning that up to 4 agents can be active at once, including you.

Full-history forks (`fork_turns` omitted or `"all"`) inherit the parent model and reasoning effort and do not accept overrides. [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 18.0 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"840b71","wall_time_seconds":0.4158304,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":180,"output":"src\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\content\\\\mixedDrillItems.ts\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\content\\\\mixedDrillItems.test.ts\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\StagedReasoningDrill.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\StagedReasoningDrill.test.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningMixedDrill.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\OpeningMixedDrill.test.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\MixedFamilyPractice.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\conditionals\\\\components\\\\MixedFamilyPractice.test.tsx\\nsrc\\\\bootcamps\\\\main-conclusion\\\\modules\\\\drills\\\\DrillMixedClassification.tsx\\nsrc\\\\features\\\\conditional-refresher\\\\ConditionalIndicatorsRefresher.tsx\\nsrc\\\\features\\\\conditional-refresher\\\\ConditionalIndicatorsRefresher.test.tsx\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"b1196a","wall_time_seconds":0.4164004,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":0,"output":""}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"f31e2d","wall_time_seconds":0.3914585,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":3340,"output":"Warning: truncated output (original token count: 3340)\\nTotal output lines: 149\\n\\n---\\r\\ntitle: Teaching Voice\\r\\ntype: concept\\r\\ntags: [lsat-u, voice, pedagogy, content]\\r\\ncreated: 2026-06-10\\r\\nupdated: 2026-10-02\\r\\nstatus: active\\r\\nsubject: Teaching Voice\\r\\naliases: []\\r\\n---\\r\\n\\r\\n# Teaching Voice\\r\\n\\r\\nHow LSAT U sounds, across every surface. This is the canonical voice doc: route all student-facing and brand copy through it. Built from a grill with Joshua (2026-06-13), then tested and enriched against the full corpus of his real correspondence (944 messages he authored, mid-2025 to mid-2026, from the Gmail takeout; 2026-07-18). Claims below marked with a quote are evidenced by that corpus.\\r\\n\\r\\nIt replaces the old two-register / 15-named-tools \\"preserve verbatim\\" profile (deleted 2026-06-10) and folds in the brand/marketing register the earlier draft was missing.\\r\\n\\r\\n**LSAT teaching standard, selected 2026-10-02:** Joshua chose the supplied Conditional Reasoning transcription as the reference for how LSAT material should sound and unfold. Read [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md) before drafting lessons, notes, explanations, drills, flashcards, quizzes, or instructional feedback. It contains the detailed source analysis, original calibration examples, progression, and review rubric. [Source identity and limits](sources/conditional-reasoning-voice.md) are preserved separately. This choice develops the teaching-content register below; correspondence and marketing retain their own registers.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Core voice (every register)\\r\\n\\r\\nThe constant under all three registers below. This is Joshua on the page.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Quiet authority.** State the value and let it land. Never oversell, never beg, never perform enthusiasm.\\r\\n- **Specific, never generic.** Real scores, the actual skill, the student\'s own history. Praise is a specific observation, not \\"great job.\\"\\r\\n- **Honest about the work, gentle about the person.** Be blunt that the LSAT is hard (\\"expect this to be exhausting and very challenging\\"). Never be blunt about a student\'s shortcomings. Honesty points at the material; warmth points at the person. When someone slips, nudge gently, never accuse.\\r\\n- **Light, grounded metaphor, understated in email.** In his real correspondence the recurring image is quiet: \\"partner with you on your LSAT journey,\\" \\"getting set up,\\" \\"foundational.\\" The vivid images (runway, climb, march toward November, an easy door to walk through) belong to authored teaching and brand copy, not to email. Keep metaphor light and grounded; never float off into abstraction.\\r\\n- **Balanced rhythm.** Air between ideas. Real correspondence is short and single-purpose (median ~280 characters). In teaching, vary sentence and paragraph length with the reasoning: give an explanation room, then let a short sentence settle the consequence. Do not impose email brevity on a lesson or cut its intermediate reasoning.\\r\\n- **Plain professional diction.** No corporate jargon, no startup hype (\\"unlock,\\" \\"crush,\\" \\"level up,\\" \\"game-changer\\"; \\"crush\\" appears once in 944 messages). Contractions are the norm, not the exception. Exclamation points are genuine, not performed: a warm message often carries one, occasionally more, especially in openers (\\"Sorry for such a long delay!\\"). He is not exclamation-averse.\\r\\n- **Forward-looking.** End on the next step or what\'s ahead.\\r\\n- **No em dashes, ever.** Use periods, colons before lists, the middot (·) as a separator, and numbered steps for procedures.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Openings, closings, and signature phrases\\r\\n\\r\\nThe mechanical fingerprint of his email, from the real corpus. Match these when drafting as Joshua.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Greeting.** \\"Hi [Name],\\" is the default (used ~519x). \\"Hey [Name],\\" for established/returning students (~229x, warmer). \\"Hello [Name],\\" (~105x) and \\"Dear [Name],\\" (~18x, formal) for first contact or a more formal note.\\r\\n- **Sign-off.** \\"Best,\\" and \\"Best regards,\\" are the workhorses (~360x / ~237x). \\"Thanks,\\" when it\'s a favor or logistics (~146x). \\"Warmly,\\" / \\"Warm regards,\\" (~54x) for a genuinely warm no…1340 tokens truncated…nd demanding situation.\\r\\n\\r\\n## The three registers\\r\\n\\r\\nWhich voice governs which surface.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 1. Coaching (earnest) -- inbox, personal encouragement, email\\r\\n\\r\\nFully earnest. Warm, sincere, calm, patient. No jokes. The closest thing to being in the room with Joshua. Normalizes struggle, then moves to the work.\\r\\n\\r\\nReference line (target tone):\\r\\n\\r\\n> Totally normal to trip on this one. Let\'s slow down: what is the author actually concluding? Once you see it, the trap answers lose their grip. You\'ve got this.\\r\\n\\r\\n- **Encouragement (student missed a hard one):** honest first, then forward. Name that it\'s genuinely hard, stay calm, move to the work. Not soft-reassuring, not cold.\\r\\n  > Missing this is expected. It\'s one of the hardest moves on the test. Let\'s slow down and see where it slipped.\\r\\n- **Accountability (student isn\'t doing the work):** gentle nudge, low pressure. Frame it as a slip, find a rhythm together. Never \\"the reps aren\'t happening,\\" never tough love.\\r\\n- **Praise (student nails it):** name the exact skill, tie it to their history, raise the bar.\\r\\n  > That\'s the cleanest you\'ve split premise from conclusion yet. Let\'s push it into the harder sets.\\r\\n- **Hard truth about the test:** state it plainly. \\"Do not expect for it to come easy. Expect to find this exhausting.\\"\\r\\n\\r\\n### 2. Teaching content (conversational reasoning) -- lessons, recaps, notes, drills, explanations\\r\\n\\r\\nUse [LSAT teaching voice standard](../references/lsat-teaching-voice-standard.md), Joshua\'s selected calibration from 2026-10-02. Keep the reader beside the teacher. Explain the ordinary meaning, ground it in an original example, make the small inferences audible, ask a useful question, and show both the consequence and its limit. Introduce terminology and notation when their meaning is established. Give each concept enough development to be learned from the page.\\r\\n\\r\\nContractions and varied sentence lengths should sound natural when read aloud. Repetition should change the reader\'s view of the same relationship. Questions should give the reader actual thinking work. Light humor may appear within an explanation when it helps understanding; this supersedes the older wit-only-at-the-edges restriction. Do not force punchlines, slang, hyperbole, catchphrases, or paragraph quotas.\\r\\n\\r\\nThe source governs delivery, not Joshua\'s procedure, indicator taxonomy, biography, or the scope of a lesson. Preserve his approved methods and logical precision. Personal encouragement still follows the earnest coaching register. Project-owned `lsat-u-voice` routes here and to the detailed standard; an older vendor writer-blend skill must not override this selection.\\r\\n\\r\\n### 3. Brand (sharp wit) -- landing, auth, marketing\\r\\n\\r\\nConfident and clever, the way a good lawyer is clever. Wit tied to the real skill, not wordplay for its own sake. Playful is allowed, but every clever line must be earned and true. Hollow cleverness fails (the killed \\"the next card is the page in front of you\\" was clever but empty).\\r\\n\\r\\n> Most prep teaches the test. We teach the tell.\\r\\n\\r\\nBrand copy speaks as the platform (\\"we\\", \\"you\\"), not as an individual (\\"I\\", \\"Joshua\\"). The product is the coach; no single person narrates the marketing.\\r\\n\\r\\n## Don\'t\\r\\n\\r\\n- No cold-testing or gotcha tone. (Ties to teach-first-calibrate-after.)\\r\\n- No condescension or empty cheerleading. Encouragement must sit on real substance.\\r\\n- No generic praise, no hollow cleverness, no hedging (\\"you might want to\\").\\r\\n- No hype/startup vocabulary.\\r\\n- No em dashes, ever.\\r\\n\\r\\nCorrections log: [[COPY-CORRECTIONS]] records every student-facing wording fix and\\r\\nthe reason it was wrong. Read it before writing or editing LSAT U copy.\\r\\n\\r\\nRelated: teach-first-calibrate-after, LSAT U. Source samples: `raw/templates/Golden Email Template Playbook.md`, `raw/templates/email_writing_examples_11_full.txt`, and the full authored-email corpus from the Gmail takeout (944 messages, analyzed 2026-07-18; takeout at `raw/sources/private/takeout/`, git-ignored).\\r\\n"}'}]

`NOT (${text})` : text;\\r\\n/** Draws each NOT (...) in an option as the struck idea the lesson uses, with a hidden \\"not\\" for screen readers. */\\r\\nfunction OptionText({ text }: { text: string }) {\\r\\n  return <>{text.split(/(NOT \\\\([^)]*\\\\))/g).map((part, index) => part.startsWith(\'NOT (\') ? \'Correct.\'; }\\r\\nfunction choices(name: string, prompt: string, options: string[], correct: number, explanation: string, hints?: string[]): Stage { return { name, prompt, options, correct, explanation, hints }; }\\r\\nconst FROM_REQUIREMENT = \'That is what the rule requires. Check the complete conditions and arrow direction.');\\n  expect(status).not.toHaveTextContent('Conditional:');\");p.write_text(s)\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/mixedDrillItems.test.ts');s=p.read_text().replace('Anyone who receives a wristband','Receiving a wristband');p.write_text(s)\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/OpeningMixedDrill.test.tsx');s=p.read_text();s=s.replace(\"import { mixedFamilyItems }\", \"import { CourseProgressProvider, type CourseProgressStore } from '../courseProgress';\\nimport { mixedFamilyItems }\")\ns+='''\n\nit('restores checked choices and unfinished independent writing after a remount', () => {\n  let saved: CourseProgressStore['steps'] = {};\n  const save: CourseProgressStore['save'] = (id, patch) => { saved = { ...saved, [id]: { ...saved[id], ...patch, updatedAt: '2026-10-13' } as CourseProgressStore['steps'][string] }; };\n  const mountSaved = () => render(<CourseProgressProvider store={{ steps: saved, save }}><OpeningMixedDrill stepId=\"mixed-practice\"/></CourseProgressProvider>);\n  let view = mountSaved();\n  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Begin practice' }));\n  for (let index = 0; index < 10; index++) { solve(index); next(); }\n  fireEvent.change(active().getByLabelText('Your diagram'), { target: { value: 'My unfinished diagram' } });\n  fireEvent.change(active().getByLabelText('Read it in English'), { target: { value: 'My unfinished sentence' } });\n  view.unmount();\n  view = mountSaved();\n  expect(active().getByLabelText('Your diagram')).toHaveValue('My unfinished diagram');\n  expect(active().getByLabelText('Read it in English')).toHaveValue('My unfinished sentence');\n  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' })).toBeDisabled();\n  fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Previous question' }));\n  expect(active().getByRole('status')).toHaveTextContent('Conditional:');\n  expect(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next question' })).toBeEnabled();\n  for (let index = 0; index < 9; index++) fireEvent.click(screen.getByRole('button', { name: 'Previous question' }));\n  expect(active().getByRole('button', { name: completeConditionItems[0].options[completeConditionItems[0].correct] })).toHaveAttribute('aria-pressed', 'true');\n  expect(active().getByRole('status')).toHaveTextContent(completeConditionItems[0].feedback[completeConditionItems[0].correct]);\n}, 30000);\n''';p.write_text(s)\np=Path('src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.test.tsx');s=p.read_text();s+='''\n\nit('keeps the next question locked until both checked choices are correct', () => {\n  const view = render(<MixedFamilyPractice/>);\n  const scope = within(view.container.querySelector<HTMLElement>('[data-mixed-item=\"1\"]')!);\n  const item = mixedFamilyItems[0];\n  fireEvent.change(scope.getByLabelText('Your diagram for sentence 1'), { target: { value: 'My diagram' } });\n  fireEvent.change(scope.getByLabelText('Your English readback for sentence 1'), { target: { value: 'My readback' } });\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: 'Compare my translation for sentence 1' }));\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: item.target }));\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: item.right }));\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check question 1' }));\n  expect(view.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next mixed question' })).toBeDisabled();\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: item.left }));\n  fireEvent.click(scope.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check question 1' }));\n  expect(view.getByRole('button', { name: 'Next mixed question' })).toBeEnabled();\n});\n''';p.write_text(s)\n'@ | python -",workdir:"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os-projects\\lsatu-conditional-takeover",max_output_tokens:1000}));

## assistant

Fixed the owned mixed and staged drills in `lsatu-conditional-takeover`.

- Mixed practice now restores writing, choices, diagrams and checked feedback after reload.
- Saved work is fingerprinted; outdated attempts are archived and incompatible checks restart.
- Wrong mixed answers cannot advance. Finishing redirects to any unfinished question.
- Equivalence and obligation mistakes now receive separate fresh checks when both need repair.
- Diagram connectors lock after checking, and wrong embedded diagrams provide a hint without exposing the answer.
- Staged completion reports revised work as started and cannot finish with unfinished questions.
- Corrected stale inherited tests and misleading family-reveal instructions.

31 focused tests passed across five files. OpeningMixed required the thread pool to avoid a Windows worker RPC timeout; its clean run passed all 13 then-existing tests, and the additional diagnostic regression passed separately. Root owns final typecheck, build and browser acceptance. No commits or pushes.

\'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'lessons\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><BookOpen size={18}/>Lessons</Link>\\r\\n    <p>Practice</p><Link aria-current={view === \'practice\' ? \'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'practice\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><Sparkles size={18}/>Practice Drills</Link>\\r\\n    <Link aria-current={view === \'labs\' ? \'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'labs\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><Dumbbell size={18}/>Drill Lab</Link>\\r\\n    <p>Tools</p><Link aria-current={view === \'tools\' ? \'page\' : undefined} to={url(\'tools\')} onClick={closeForNavigation}><Bookmark size={18}/>Flashcards</Link>\\r\\n  </nav>;\\r\\n  return <>\\r\\n    <aside className=\\"oc-course-rail\\"><Link className=\\"oc-back\\" to=\\"/classroom\\"><ArrowLeft size={15}/>Classroom</Link><div className=\\"oc-brand\\">Conditional logic</div>{navigation}</aside>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"oc-mobile-nav\\"><Link to={url(\'lessons\')}>Conditional logic</Link><Sheet open={open} onOpenChange={setOpen}><SheetTrigger asChild><button type=\\"button\\"><Menu size={18}/>Course menu</button></SheetTrigger><SheetContent side=\\"left\\" className=\\"oc-course-drawer\\" aria-describedby={undefined} onCloseAutoFocus={event => {\\r\\n      if (!focusDestination.current) return;\\r\\n      event.preventDefault();\\r\\n      focusDestination.current = false;\\r\\n      requestAnimationFrame(() => {\\r\\n        const heading = document.querySelector<HTMLElement>(\'.oc-course-screen [data-course-heading] h1\');\\r\\n        if (heading) { heading.tabIndex = -1; heading.focus({ preventScroll: true }); }\\r\\n      });\\r\\n    }}><SheetTitle>Conditional logic</SheetTitle>{navigation}</SheetContent></Sheet></div>\\r\\n  </>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nfunction CourseCard({ step, url }: { step: OpeningCourseStep; url: OpeningCourseUrl }) {\\r\\n  const saved = useCourseProgress()?.steps[step.id];\\r\\n"}'}]

const require = (condition, message) => { if (!condition) throw new Error(message); };\n  const report = [];\n  for (const mode of ['sufficient', 'contrapositive']) {\n    const destination = new b').innerText()).trim() === '01', `${mode}: requires a fresh first question`);\n    require((await runner.locator('.sr-step-label').innerText()).includes('STEP 1 '), `${mode}: requires a fresh first step`);\n    let drafts = 0;\n    for (let i = 0; i < items.length; i++) {\n      for (let s = 0; s < items[i].stages.length; s++) {\n        const stage = items[i].stages[s];\n        require((await name: /Next step|Next question|Review this set/ });\n        if (stage.options) {\n          const options = runner.getByRole('group', { name: 'Answer choices' }).getByRole('button');\n          if (i === 0 && s === 0) {\n            require(await next.isDisabled(), `${mode}: unchecked answer advanced`);\n            const wrong = (stage.correct + 1) % stage.options.length;\n            for (let attempt = 0; attempt < 2; attempt++) {\n              await options.nth(wrong).click();\n              await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step', exact: true }).click();\n              require(await next.isDisabled(), `${mode}: wrong answer advanced`);\n              require((await runner.locator('.sr-feedback p').innerText()).trim().length > 5, `${mode}: wrong hint missing`);\n              await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'Try again', exact: true }).click();\n            }\n          }\n          await options.nth(stage.correct).click();\n          await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'Check this step', exact: true }).click();\n        } else {\n          const written = `My independent readback: ${stage.model}`;\n          require(await runner.getByRole('button', { name: /Compare my sentence|Compare my explanation/ }).isDisabled(), `${mode}: empty writing compared`);\n          await runner.getByRole('textbox').fill(written);\n          if (drafts === 0) {\n            await page.reload();\n            await runner.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n            require(await runner.getByRole('textbox').inputValue() === written, `${mode}: draft lost after reload`);\n          }\n          await runner.getByRole('button', { name: /Compare my sentence|Compare my explanation/ }).click();\n          require(await next.isDisabled(), `${mode}: self-review was credited automatically`);\n          require((await runner.locator('.sr-model').innerText()).includes(stage.model), `${mode}: model changed meaning`);\n          await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'I compared the direction and complete conditions', exact: true }).click();\n          drafts++;\n        }\n        require(await next.isEnabled(), `${mode} question ${i + 1} step ${s + 1}: correct check did not enable next`);\n        await next.click();\n      }\n    }\n    await runner.getByRole('heading', { name: 'You worked through the set.' }).waitFor();\n    require((await runner.locator('.sr-results').innerText()).includes(`${items.length} of ${items.length} questions completed`), `${mode}: result total incorrect`);\n    await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'Question 1', exact: true }).click();\n    require((await runner.locator('.sr-feedback').innerText()).length > 5, `${mode}: review lost checked response`);\n    await page.reload();\n    require((await runner.locator('.sr-feedback').innerText()).length > 5, `${mode}: review lost response after reload`);\n    const indexUrl = new URL(base); indexUrl.searchParams.set('page', 'lessons'); indexUrl.searchParams.delete('run');\n    await page.goto(indexUrl.href);\n    const card = page.locator(`.oc-course-path li:has(a[href*=\"page=${mode}-practice\"])`);\n    await card.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n    require((await card.innerText()).includes('Done'), `${mode}: course card is not Done`);\n    report.push({ mode, questions: items.length, writtenComparisons: drafts, wrongHints: 2, reload: true, review: true, status: 'Done' });\n  }\n  return drill = await import('/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/OpeningMixedDrill.tsx');\n    const content = await import('/src/bootcamps/conditionals/content/mixedDrillItems.ts');\n    const independent = await import('/src/bootcamps/conditionals/components/MixedFamilyPractice.tsx');\n    return { complete: content.completeConditionItems, diagrams: drill.openingDiagramItems, independent: independent.mixedFamilyItems, equivalence: content.equivalenceItems, transfer: content.transferItems, fresh: content.confirmationItemsByError.qualification[0] };\n  });\n  const require = (condition, message) => { if (!condition) throw new Error(message); };\n  const url = new URL(base); url.searchParams.set('page', 'mixed-practice'); url.searchParams.set('run', '1');\n  await page.goto(url.href);\n  const runner = page.locator('.oc-course-body:not([hidden]) .omd-runner');\n  await runner.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n  const intro = async () => {\n    const introduction = runner.locator('.omd-introduction:not([hidden])');\n    if (await introduction.count()) await introduction.getByRole('button', { name: /Begin practice|Continue/, exact: true }).click();\n  };\n  await intro();\n  const active = () => runner.locator('[data-mixed-question]:not([hidden])');\n  const next = () => runner.locator('.omd-navigation').getByRole('button', { name: /Next question|Try a fresh case|Review practice|Finish practice/, exact: true });\n  require((await runner.locator('.omd-progress-heading').innerText()).includes('Question 1 of 24'), 'Mixed requires a fresh first question');\n  const questions = [...authored.complete.map(item => ({ kind: 'choice', item })), ...authored.diagrams.map(item => ({ kind: 'diagram', item })), ...authored.independent.map(item => ({ kind: 'independent', item })), ...authored.equivalence.map(item => ({ kind: 'choice', item })), ...authored.transfer.map(item => ({ kind: 'choice', item }))];\n  for (let index = 0; index < questions.length; index++) {\n    const { kind, item } = questions[index];\n    if (kind === 'choice') {\n      if (index === 0) {\n        await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.options[0], exact: true }).click();\n        await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer', exact: true }).click();\n        require(await next().isDisabled(), 'Mixed wrong choice advanced');\n        await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Try this question again', exact: true }).click();\n      }\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.options[item.correct], exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer', exact: true }).click();\n    } else if (kind === 'diagram') {\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Choose the left condition', exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.left, exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Choose the right condition', exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.right, exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer', exact: true }).click();\n    } else {\n      require(await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Compare with the choices', exact: true }).isDisabled(), 'Independent blank writing exposed choices');\n      const draft = `${item.left} → ${item.right}`;\n      await active().getByLabel('Your diagram', { exact: true }).fill(draft);\n      await active().getByLabel('Read it in English', { exact: true }).fill(item.readback);\n      if (index === 10) {\n        await page.reload(); await runner.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n        require(await active().getByLabel('Your diagram', { exact: true }).inputValue() === draft, 'Independent draft lost after reload');\n        require(await active().getByLabel('Read it in English', { exact: true }).inputValue() === item.readback, 'Independent English draft lost after reload');\n        require(await next().isDisabled(), 'Unfinished restored draft enabled next');\n      }\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Compare with the choices', exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.target, exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: item.left, exact: true }).click();\n      await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer', exact: true }).click();\n    }\n    require(await next().isEnabled(), `Mixed question ${index + 1} correct answer did not enable next`);\n    await next().click(); await intro();\n  }\n  require((await active().innerText()).includes(authored.fresh.sentence), 'Fresh qualification follow-up missing');\n  await active().getByRole('button', { name: authored.fresh.options[authored.fresh.correct], exact: true }).click();\n  await active().getByRole('button', { name: 'Check answer', exact: true }).click();\n  await next().click();\n  await runner.getByRole('heading', { name: 'Review your practice', exact: true }).waitFor();\n  require((await runner.locator('.omd-summary').innerText()).includes('23 of 24 correctly on your first try'), 'First-check score changed after repair');\n  await page.reload(); await runner.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n  await runner.getByRole('button', { name: 'Review my review');\n  const indexUrl = new URL(base); indexUrl.searchParams.set('page', 'lessons'); indexUrl.searchParams.delete('run');\n  await page.goto(indexUrl.href);\n  const card = page.locator('.oc-course-path li:has(a[href*=\"page=mixed-practice\"])'); await card.waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n  require((await card.innerText()).includes('Done'), 'Mixed course card not [{'type': 'input_text', 'text': 'Script completed\nWall time 16.7 seconds\nOutput:\n'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"e26d76","wall_time_seconds":0.9212718,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":338,"output":"import React, { createContext, useContext, useEffect, useState } from \'react\';\\r\\n\\r\\nexport type Theme = \'dark\' | \'light\';\\r\\n\\r\\ninterface ThemeContextValue {\\r\\n  theme: Theme;\\r\\n  toggleTheme: () => void;\\r\\n  setTheme: (t: Theme) => void;\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nconst ThemeContext = createContext<ThemeContextValue>({\\r\\n  theme: \'dark\',\\r\\n  toggleTheme: () => {},\\r\\n  setTheme: () => {},\\r\\n});\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function ThemeProvider({ children }: { children: React.ReactNode }) {\\r\\n  const [theme, setThemeState] = useState<Theme>(() => {\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      const saved = localStorage.getItem(\'lsatu-theme\');\\r\\n      return (saved === \'dark\' ? \'dark\' : \'light\') as Theme;\\r\\n    } catch {\\r\\n      return \'light\';\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  });\\r\\n\\r\\n  useEffect(() => {\\r\\n    const root = document.documentElement;\\r\\n    if (theme === \'light\') {\\r\\n      root.classList.add(\'light\');\\r\\n    } else {\\r\\n      root.classList.remove(\'light\');\\r\\n    }\\r\\n    try {\\r\\n      localStorage.setItem(\'lsatu-theme\', theme);\\r\\n    } catch {\\r\\n      /* ignore */\\r\\n    }\\r\\n  }, [theme]);\\r\\n\\r\\n  const setTheme = (t: Theme) => setThemeState(t);\\r\\n  const toggleTheme = () => setThemeState(t => (t === \'dark\' ? \'light\' : \'dark\'));\\r\\n\\r\\n  return (\\r\\n    <ThemeContext.Provider value={{ theme, toggleTheme, setTheme }}>\\r\\n      {children}\\r\\n    </ThemeContext.Provider>\\r\\n  );\\r\\n}\\r\\n\\r\\nexport function useTheme() {\\r\\n  return useContext(ThemeContext);\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}, {'type': 'input_text', 'text': '{"chunk_id":"1a866c","wall_time_seconds":0.5110159,"exit_code":0,"original_token_count":208,"output":"20:  { id: \'indicators\', title: \'Indicator words\', description: \'Group 1 or Group 2? Pick for each word.\' },\\r\\n];\\r\\nexport function FlashcardTools({ url, allowed }: { url: OpeningCourseUrl; allowed: string[] }) {\\r\\n  const [params] = useSearchParams();\\r\\n  // The word deck mixes both groups. Old Group 1 and Group 2 links land on it.\\r\\n  const available = [...allowed.filter(id => id === \'foundation\'), ...(allowed.some(id => id === \'group-1\' || id === \'group-2\') ? [\'indicators\'] : [])];\\r\\n  const requested = [\'group-1\', \'group-2\'].includes(params.get(\'deck\') ?? \'\') ? \'indicators\' : params.get(\'deck\');\\r\\n  const set = sets.find(item => item.id === requested && available.includes(item.id));\\r\\n  if (!set) return <div className=\\"oc-course-note co-flashcard-tools\\">\\r\\n    <header data-course-heading><p className=\\"oc-label\\">Tools</p><h1>Flashcards</h1></header>\\r\\n    <div className=\\"co-flashcard-sets\\">{sets.filter(item => available.includes(item.id)).map(item => <section key={item.id}><h2>{item.title}</h2><p>{item.description}</p><Link className=\\"oc-action\\" to={flashcardUrl(url, item.id)}>Go to flashcards<ArrowRight size={16} aria-hidden=\\"true\\"/></Link></section>)}</div>\\r\\n    {!available.length && <p>No flashcards are available in this work.</p>}\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n  if (set.id === \'indicators\') return <div className=\\"oc-course-note co-flashcard-tools\\">\\r\\n    <nav className=\\"co-flashcard-return\\" aria-label=\\"Flashcard navigation\\"><Link to={url(\'tools\')}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>All flashcard sets</Link>{(params.get(\'from\') === \'sufficient\' || params.get(\'from\') === \'necessary\') && <Link to={url(params.get(\'from\')!)}>Back to {params.get(\'from\') === \'sufficient\' ? \'sufficient\' : \'necessary\'} condition<ArrowRight size={16}/></Link>}</nav>\\r\\n    <header data-course-heading><h1>{set.title}</h1></header>\\r\\n    <IndicatorWordDeck/>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n  return <div className=\\"oc-course-note co-flashcard-tools\\">\\r\\n    <nav className=\\"co-flashcard-return\\" aria-label=\\"Flashcard navigation\\"><Link to={url(\'tools\')}><ArrowLeft size={16}/>All flashcard sets</Link></nav>\\r\\n    <header data-course-heading><h1>{set.title}</h1></header>\\r\\n    <TermDeck/>\\r\\n  </div>;\\r\\n}\\r\\n"}'}]

text(await tools.exec_command({cmd:"@'\nfrom pathlib import Path\np=Path('output/playwright/conditional-takeover')\np.joinpath('visual-matrix.js').write_text('''async (page) => {\n  const base = new URL(page.url());\n  const steps = await page.evaluate(async () => (await import('/src/bootcamps/conditionals/openingCoursePath.ts')).OPENING_COURSE_PATH);\n  const widths = [375, 640, 900, 1440];\n  const routes = [{ id: 'lessons' }, ...steps.flatMap(step => step.type === 'drill' ? [{ id: step.id }, { id: step.id, run: true }] : [{ id: step.id }]), { id: 'tools' }, { id: 'tools', deck: 'foundation' }, { id: 'tools', deck: 'indicators' }, { id: 'tools', deck: 'group-1' }, { id: 'tools', deck: 'group-2' }];\n  const representative = ['necessary', 'must-arrow', 'source-rephrase-contrapositive', 'mixed-practice', 'sufficient'];\n  const failures = [], screenshots = [], checks = [];\n  const navigate = async (route, theme) => {\n    const url = new URL(base); url.searchParams.set('page', route.id); url.searchParams.delete('run'); url.searchParams.delete('deck');\n    if (route.run) url.searchParams.set('run', '1'); if (route.deck) url.searchParams.set('deck', route.deck);\n    await page.goto(url.href);\n    await page.locator('.oc-course-screen').waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n    await page.evaluate(theme => document.documentElement.classList.toggle('light', theme === 'light'), theme);\n    // Wait for layout and font metrics, without changing learner progress.\n    await page.evaluate(async () => { await document.fonts.ready; await new Promise(resolve => requestAnimationFrame(() => requestAnimationFrame(resolve))); });\n  };\n  const inspect = async () => page.evaluate(() => {\n    const width = document.documentElement.clientWidth;\n    const visible = element => { const r = element.getBoundingClientRect(); const s = getComputedStyle(element); return r.width > 0 && r.height > 0 && s.visibility !== 'hidden' && s.display !== 'none'; };\n    const h = [...document.querySelectorAll('h1')].filter(visible).map(element => element.textContent.trim()).filter(Boolean);\n    const controls = [...document.querySelectorAll('.oc-course-screen button, .oc-course-screen input, .oc-course-screen textarea, .oc-course-screen a')].filter(visible);\n    const clipped = controls.filter(element => {\n      const r = element.getBoundingClientRect();\n      if (r.left >= -2 && r.right <= width + 2) return false;\n      // A horizontally scrollable palette is reachable; document-level overflow is not.\n      for (let parent = element.parentElement; parent && parent !== document.body; parent = parent.parentElement) {\n        if (['auto', 'scroll'].includes(getComputedStyle(parent).overflowX) && parent.scrollWidth > parent.clientWidth + 2) return false;\n      }\n      return true;\n    }).map(element => ({ text: (element.getAttribute('aria-label') || element.textContent || '').trim().slice(0, 100), tag: element.tagName, left: Math.round(element.getBoundingClientRect().left), right: Math.round(element.getBoundingClientRect().right) }));\n    return { heading: h, viewport: width, documentWidth: document.documentElement.scrollWidth, overflow: document.documentElement.scrollWidth > width + 2, clipped, controlCount: controls.length, themeClass: document.documentElement.classList.contains('light') ? 'light' : 'dark' };\n  });\n  for (const width of widths) {\n    await page.setViewportSize({ width, height: 900 });\n    for (const route of routes) {\n      await navigate(route, 'light');\n      const result = await inspect();\n      const record = { width, page: route.id, run: !!route.run, deck: route.deck, theme: 'light', ...result };\n      checks.push(record);\n      if (!result.heading.length || result.overflow || result.clipped.length) failures.push(record);\n      const screenshotTarget = representative.includes(route.id) && (route.id !== 'source-rephrase-contrapositive' && route.id !== 'mixed-practice' || route.run);\n      if (screenshotTarget && (width === 375 || width === 1440)) {\n        const path = `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-takeover/output/playwright/conditional-takeover/visual-${route.id}-${width}-light.png`;\n        await page.screenshot({ path, fullPage: true }); screenshots.push(path);\n      }\n    }\n  }\n  for (const route of [{ id: 'necessary' }, { id: 'mixed-practice', run: true }]) {\n    await page.setViewportSize({ width: 640, height: 900 }); await navigate(route, 'dark');\n    const result = await inspect(); const record = { width: 640, page: route.id, run: !!route.run, theme: 'dark', ...result };\n    checks.push(record); if (!result.heading.length || result.overflow || result.clipped.length) failures.push(record);\n    const path = `C:/Users/Joshu/dev/ai-os-projects/lsatu-conditional-takeover/output/playwright/conditional-takeover/visual-${route.id}-640-dark.png`;\n    await page.screenshot({ path, fullPage: true }); screenshots.push(path);\n  }\n  await page.evaluate(() => document.documentElement.classList.add('light'));\n  return { authoredCourseRoutes: steps.length, viewports: widths, checkedSurfaces: checks.length, failures, screenshots, checks };\n}\n''',encoding='utf-8')\np.joinpath('media-live.js').write_text('''async (page) => {\n  const base = new URL(page.url());\n  const results = [];\n  for (const lesson of ['sufficient', 'necessary']) {\n    const url = new URL(base); url.searchParams.set('page', lesson); url.searchParams.delete('run');\n    await page.goto(url.href); await page.locator('.oc-course-body:not([hidden])').waitFor({ state: 'visible' });\n    const videos = page.locator('.oc-course-body:not([hidden]) video');\n    if (!(await videos.count())) results.push({ lesson, error: 'No teaching video exists on this lesson' });\n    for (let index = 0; index < await videos.count(); index++) {\n      const video = videos.nth(index); await video.scrollIntoViewIfNeeded();\n      // A real pointer interaction precedes playback. No authored source or learner record is changed.\n      await video.click({ position: { x: 15, y: 15 } });\n      const result = await video.evaluate(async element => {\n        const source = element.currentSrc || element.querySelector('source')?.src || element.src;\n        const startingTime = element.currentTime;\n        const oldMuted = element.muted; element.muted = true;\n        try {\n          await element.play();\n          await new Promise((resolve, reject) => {\n            const timeout = setTimeout(() => { cleanup(); reject(new Error('Playback did not advance within 8 seconds')); }, 8000);\n            const check = () => { if (element.readyState >= 2 && element.currentTime > startingTime + 0.05) { cleanup(); resolve(); } };\n            const fail = () => { cleanup(); reject(new Error(element.error?.message || 'Video error')); };\n            const cleanup = () => { clearTimeout(timeout); element.removeEventListener('timeupdate', check); element.removeEventListener('error', fail); };\n            element.addEventListener('timeupdate', check); element.addEventListener('error', fail); check();\n          });\n          return { source, duration: element.duration, readyState: element.readyState, advanced: element.currentTime - startingTime, played: true };\n        } catch (error) { return { source, played: false, error: String(error), readyState: element.readyState }; }\n        finally { element.pause(); element.muted = oldMuted; }\n      });\n      results.push({ lesson, index, ...result });\n    }\n  }\n  return results;\n}\n''',encoding='utf-8')\n'@ | python -",workdir:"C:\\Users\\Joshu\\dev\\ai-os-projects\\lsatu-conditional-takeover",max_output_tokens:1000}));
